## C. Builder-process defects (mine, not Brain's)

Recorded because they cost the owner real time and must not repeat.

| ID | Date | What I did wrong | Consequence | Correction |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **BLD-019** | introduced 09-07 ~06:00 by my own [BLD-016] → found 09-07 13:35 while answering *"is it running since 5h?"* | Fixed the ingest GATE to take its count from the header (8 capabilities) and **left the PRODUCER emitting 9**. `score-schema.json` still declared `minItems/maxItems: 9` and `score.md` still said *"the nine capabilities"*, so the scorer dutifully emitted nine slots with **Self-Pruning listed twice** for the merged slot | **Every automatic scoring run was rejected from the moment I fixed the gate.** Iteration 75 logged `--ingest: expected 8 capabilities, got 9` → *"ITERATION 75 FAILED — nothing deployed, no capability movement (streak 78)"*. I had hit this exact mismatch hours earlier and worked around it by hand-normalising the card 9→8 to get the 54.5 reading in — then fixed the consumer and never asked what produced it. **A gate and its producer are one contract; fixing the half that complains is how you convert a silent wrong answer into a loud no answer** | Producer now emits **eight, each name exactly once**, with the reason written into the schema description so the next reader does not re-derive it. This was the **third** file the 2026-08-31 commit *"nine become eight everywhere the machinery reads them"* did not reach — `scorecard.py` was the second ([BLD-016]). Landed at 13:36, while a pulse was live and before its SCORE phase, because prompts and schema are read from source at run time |
| **BLD-017** | in place since the panel was written → found 09-07 when the owner asked *"why still open?"* | `_finding_state()` in `pulse.py` decides whether a finding is fixed by matching strings in `brain_change_log.md`, and it recognised only two deploy wordings — `**YES — deployed` and `DEPLOYED-IN-FACT`. It matched neither the wording a human writes nor the one the deploy phase writes. It also let a `CODE-COMPLETE` verification cell **outrank an explicit DEPLOYED cell**, and treated `**NOT YET**` in the deploy column as proof that code exists | **The deck told him five things about his own system that were false, at once.** DEF-313 had read `**DEPLOYED 09-04 12:33 PKT (D-99)**` by hand for three days and rendered `open`; DEF-311 and DEF-315 carry the deploy phase's `**DEPLOYED \`sha\`**` and rendered `open`; DEF-321 shipped 09-05 and rendered `built` because its verification cell also says CODE-COMPLETE; and **DEF-319 rendered `built` when no code was ever written** — its row says *"DIAGNOSED FROM LIVE DATA AND THE CALL GRAPH, NOT FIXED"* and its commit `6dbf933` changed exactly one line, of this document. Four rows understating progress and one **overstating** it, on the panel he uses to decide what to chase | Deployed is now tested FIRST and accepts `**DEPLOYED` in any of the three wordings. `NOT FIXED` outranks `NOT YET`, because a deploy column says nothing about whether code exists. Verified per tag against the eight rows he pasted. Separately, DEF-244 (`f2d4241`) and DEF-307 (`7227181`) were confirmed by `git merge-base --is-ancestor` against the deployed `2e243a4` and their rows backfilled — they had shipped on 08-25 and 09-03 and said NOT YET ever since |
| **BLD-018** | 09-07 06:37 — **mine, and reported before he could find it** | Ran `npx vite build` in `client/` as a syntax check on a two-file JSX/CSS edit. **Apache's DocumentRoot is `/var/www/tmcai/client/dist`**, and vite empties its output directory, so that "check" replaced the live frontend bundle and destroyed the one it replaced | **An unauthorised production deploy of the whole front end**, and the previous bundle cannot be restored because vite deleted it. What is now served is HEAD's `client/src` plus my two panel files — so every committed client change since the served bundle was last built went live with it, including DEF-318's client half (`43cb17e`, 09-04) and the 08-31/09-01 capability-map and spend-deck commits. None of it is foreign code and nothing is known broken, but he did not authorise it and the rollback target no longer exists | **`client/dist` is the same hazard as `server/dist` and has no `chattr +i` on it.** For a JSX/CSS edit the check is `npx vite build --outDir /tmp/...` or `npx tsc`/lint — never a default-output build inside a served tree. **Rule: before running any build, establish where its output goes and who serves it.** CLAUDE.md's dist immutability taught this for the server and I did not carry it across one directory |
| **BLD-016** | 08-31 → found 09-07 on the first card the repaired scorer produced | The 08-31 commit is titled *"capability merge: nine become eight **everywhere the machinery reads them**"*. It did not reach `scorecard.py`, whose ingest gate is a literal `if len(caps_in) != 9`. So the header carries eight capabilities and the gate demanded nine | **The first correctly-merged card in the campaign was refused outright** — `--ingest: expected 9 capabilities, got 8`. Had [BLD-012] not been fixed first this would have been the *next* silent measurement failure standing behind it, and it would have read identically from outside: a scoring run that produced nothing. Two independent gates in the same pipeline, both able to kill a measurement without saying so | Count comes from the header (`len(d["capabilities"])`), which is the shape every other check in that function already asserts by name — so it survives the next merge or split instead of needing this fix again. Added a duplicate-name check in the same place: the scorer's card listed nine slots with **Self-Pruning twice** (slot 1 is the merged Simple & Smart), and the name-driven loop would have applied one score twice, silently overwriting the first |
| **BLD-015** | every automated deploy since the phase went live → found 09-07, answering *"why does the cron fix defects but never move a capability?"* | Built a deploy phase that changes production and **leaves no artifact in git**. `deployed-sha` and `deploy-history` are files nothing outside the loop reads; the chat analyst — **which is what chooses the loop's target** — reads `git log` for a deploy commit, found none, and concluded nothing had shipped. Separately, `build.md` told the Builder its target was *"the widest gap, computed from Grok's card"* and that *"Grok scored these minutes ago"*: on 09-07 the target was the 5th-widest (headroom rotation, by design), the brief named a **different** capability as WIDEST GAP twenty lines later, and the card was **162 hours old** because of [BLD-012] | **This is the mechanism behind 34 MISSES in 35 aimed iterations.** The analyst wrote, in the file the Builder is handed: *"deployed-sha was advanced to HEAD twice without a deploy; production HEAD is still `e0053d8`… DEF-321 and DEF-315 are both BUILT-NOT-DEPLOYED."* **Every word false** — the live `dist` manifest equals the one recorded for `2e243a4` and pm2 reports `tmcai-server` started 09-07 02:52:28 UTC, the second the phase restarted it. The analyst was not careless; it read the only evidence git had, and git had none. So the loop re-ranked already-shipped defects as urgent, told the Builder to *"deploy the D-102 wave"* that shipped two days earlier, and handed it a brief containing four false statements about its own aim. **A loop whose target is computed from a file that believes its fixes never ship cannot move a capability however well it builds** | One deploy commit per deploy, naming its files (never a directory — [BLD-001]), carrying the chart row and DEF rows this phase now writes; `deployed-sha` advanced to that commit so the next night does not redeploy a docs change as code. On failure it says loudly that production IS deployed and git does not know. `build.md` and `review.md` now state the target's real provenance — headroom rotation on a nightly, chat ranking on a conversational pulse, **not necessarily the widest gap** — and a new `@@CARDSTAMP@@` prints the card's age, escalating to **THIS CARD IS STALE** past 36 hours |
| **BLD-014** | every automated deploy → found 09-07, from the owner's own deck | Same shape as BLD-013, one document further along: the Builder writes *"**NOT YET at build time** (Builder does not deploy)"* into a DEF row's DEPLOYED cell — an honest note about its own moment — and **nothing ever replaced it**, because the deploy phase never wrote back to `brain_change_log.md` | **His Build Pulse deck told him "DEF-321 · alert fix built but not deployed" while DEF-321 had been live on production for two days.** The deck was reading the log correctly; the log was two days stale. DEF-311, DEF-315 and DEF-321 all shipped (D-102/103/104) with every row still reading NOT YET. This is CLAUDE.md rule 2 with no implementation on the deploy side, and its cost is not bookkeeping — it is the owner being shown shipped work as unshipped, which is precisely the "real progress looks like circling" failure the three-document protocol exists to end | `defs_deployed()` in `deploy.sh`, called on the success path only, rewrites the DEPLOYED cell for each DEF named in the deploy range — and **only when that cell still says NOT YET**. It never touches the verification column and never writes VERIFIED: deployed is not verified, and this phase cannot see the owner's traffic. Tested on a copy for idempotence, cell-count preservation and non-interference with rows outside the range; the three real rows backfilled with their true sha and timestamp |
| **BLD-012** | broke 08-31 13:00 → found 09-07 by the owner asking *"is the nightly cron improving capability at each run?"* | Wrote the scorer's dead-session recovery in `run-score.sh` against the error strings an HTTP resume failure *would* take — `not found locally`, `404 Not Found`, `Failed to restore session` — without ever running the branch against the real CLI. The CLI prints **`No conversation found with session ID: <uuid>`** and exits 1, which matches none of them | **The capability instrument was dead for seven days and nothing said so.** Twelve consecutive runs (iterations 56→73) resumed a session that no longer existed, exited non-zero, and logged one line — `grok FAILED` — that nothing counted. No `iter-NN-capability.json` was produced, so no card was ingested, so `capability_scorecard.json` froze at iteration 54 / `d144edf`; the ADVANCE test compares the last two cards, so it could never see a rise and the iteration counter froze at **73**; the deck's trend gained no column after **#54**. Meanwhile the loop went on building, reviewing and **deploying real fixes every night** (D-102/103/104, ~$321 across the last 13 nights) — aimed at a card a week old, with no measurement of whether any of it moved anything | Pattern widened to include what the tool **actually prints**, proven by a stub that emits the real message and exits 1: the recovery branch now fires, mints a new session and retries once (it had never executed). Plus `score_failed()` — a streak counter that says **"MEASUREMENT IS DEAD"** in those words from the second consecutive failure. **Rule: match on the output you have reproduced, and never let the diagnostic path be the one thing nothing exercises.** Rung 1 of Self-Healing is *notice your own failure*; the loop built to raise that capability could not do it for its own instrument. **And the deck was anchored to the wrong end** (owner, 09-07: *"we have healing pulse #73 and it is showing me up to #54?"*): the trend drew the last ten iterations THAT HAD A CARD, so it stopped at #54 and went on looking like a healthy flat trend for nineteen pulses — the absence of a measurement was the one thing the panel could not show. `capability_trend()` now ends the window at the CURRENT pulse, so a missing card renders as a column of em-dashes and the gap is the most visible thing on the card |
| **BLD-013** | every automated deploy → found 09-07 | Built the deploy phase to write the evaluation-chart row **before** the deploy, ending it with the cells *"(written before deploy — observed result appended by the deploy phase)"* and *"(pending)"* — and never wrote the code that appends them. The phase restarts production, probes it, reads the boot log, and then exits | **Half of CLAUDE.md rule 3.** The prediction was recorded so it could be wrong on the record, and the record was never closed: D-102, D-103 and D-104 all sat at `(pending)` with their evidence — manifest digest, probe seconds, matrix result — sitting unread in `loop.log`. A chart of predictions with no observations cannot answer the one question it exists for | `chart_observe()` closes the row on all three exits: deployed, rolled back, and migration-failed. The verdict it writes is **UNVERIFIED, never PROGRESS** — a healthy probe proves a live process and nothing about the owner's defect. D-102/103/104 backfilled from `loop.log` with their real numbers |
| BLD-001 | 08-05 11:40 | Used `git add server/docs/` (directory add) instead of naming files | Committed and pushed `server/docs/nexeo_self_learning&development.md` — a file AGENTS.md explicitly forbids ever committing — and it landed on production via the pull | `git rm --cached` (kept on disk), added to `.gitignore`. **Rule: always name files in `git add`, never a directory.** History still contains it; purge available on request |
| **BLD-011** | 08-31 17:31 | Wrote the llm_spend retention sweep with Prisma's ARRAY form `$transaction([insert, delete])`, in a codebase whose `db/prisma.ts` wraps `$executeRawUnsafe` — I had read that wrapper and missed what it meant for my own call. Worse: when it failed I **reported `rolledRows: 0, deletedRows: 0` and told the owner "data untouched"** | **The report was false. Both statements had already executed, un-transactionally, and 2,382,156 rows were deleted.** Building the array *calls* `$executeRawUnsafe`, and a wrapped method returns an EAGER plain promise rather than a lazy `PrismaPromise` — so both ran to completion before `$transaction` rejected the array and my catch block swallowed the error into zeroes. The atomicity I thought I had never existed on any path. **No history was lost, and that was the design, not the transaction:** the migration had already backfilled the rollup before anything was deleted, and `llm_spend_unified` serves an archived day from the rollup whether or not raw rows remain. Verified after the fact — May/June/July report **identical** calls, cost and tokens (2,374,981 calls from 1,119 rollup rows) with their raw rows physically gone | Switched to the callback form, which is the only form this codebase's wrapper re-instruments, and the test now asserts the SHAPE rather than only the ordering — ordering was already correct in the version that could not run. **Two rules.** (1) **A lazy-promise API stops being lazy the moment something wraps it; never let "I built the array" stand in for "nothing has run yet".** (2) **An error handler that returns zeroes is claiming a fact.** `catch → return {0,0}` reported "nothing happened" about a 2.38M-row delete. A handler that cannot know what completed must say so — `unknown`, not `0` — because a false zero is worse than a thrown error |
| BLD-002 | 07-28→08-04 | Carried the open-defect queue in conversation instead of a document | Made months of real progress look like circling to the owner | The three-doc protocol |
| BLD-003 | 08-04 | Reported a suite figure measured BEFORE the final edit | Claimed green while the tree was red | Re-run after the last change; quote commit-stamped numbers |
| **BLD-005** | 08-05 13:12 | Wrote a branch that deliberately bypasses another branch, and did not check what protections that branch was carrying. The DEF-035 guard skipped the confirm reducer to fix ordering — and skipped its idempotency wrapper and artifact ledger with it | Shipped a duplicate-send path to production for ~30 minutes and told the owner to run a "send" test on it. An external audit caught it, not me and not 1,272 tests | **When bypassing a code path, enumerate what it does BEYOND the thing you are bypassing.** A guard that lives inside one caller is not a guard. Structural answer: extract the chokepoint and CI-enforce a single caller, rather than duplicating the protections into the new branch |
| BLD-004 | 08-04 | Left DEF-024's fix pushed but undeployed while diagnosing other things | The same defect hit the owner three separate times | Deploy blockers before starting new diagnosis |
| **BLD-006** | 08-10 | Shipped DEF-115 and DEF-116 with code, tests and full commit messages — and **no row in this log.** Rule 2 says every change moves a row; two changes moved none, and the commit message became the only record | The exact failure BLD-002 exists to prevent, one layer down. A defect whose only record is a commit message is invisible to the recurrence table, so the next instance of its class reads as first-occurrence and gets patched again instead of structurally | Rows added retroactively 08-11 (above), marked with their real verification state. **The log row goes in the same commit as the code, not after it** — a commit message is evidence, not a ledger |
| **BLD-008** | 08-11 14:53 → caught 15:00 | Rewrote `changeStatus` to pass `actor: 'system'` **while it still accepted an `actor` argument** — every caller's identity silently discarded. **It shipped**: the collapse is in `41dfdff` and went to production in D-22 before I read my own diff | **Two failures, one line, live.** The ledger DEF-127 had just restored now records `system` for every human and agent status change through that path — so the audit trail exists and lies about who acted. And `is_owner_or_agent` treats `'system'` as always authorised, so within a tenant a non-owner could move another user's item through `POST /open-items/:id/status`, including the new CANCELLED→TRIAGED restore where that guard is the only protection. Tracked as **DEF-133** because it is a live defect, not just a process slip | Pass-through restored, threaded onto `delegateItem` and its three call sites. **Mutation-verified: re-introducing the collapse turns 3 assertions red, including a named non-owner-restore test.** **Rule: a parameter that is accepted and then ignored is worse than one never offered** — it reads as honoured at every call site, which is exactly why the diff passed review. **And the deeper one: I ran the verification matrix and shipped inside the window in which I had not yet read my own diff.** Green tests do not review intent |
| **BLD-009** | 08-11 14:53 → caught 15:05 | Introduced `INACTIVE_STATUS_VALUES` documented as *"the ONE definition of no longer on the user's plate"*, plus an `INACTIVE_STATUS_SQL` beside it — and left **all seven raw-SQL queries hand-writing their own lists**, with the SQL constant referenced by nothing at all. Also shipped in `41dfdff` | **The 7th `protection-with-two-implementations` — authored by me, in the very change meant to consolidate.** Per chart §3 this tag is *circling*, not progressing: a constant asserting it is canonical while the call sites prove it is not is precisely the comforting fiction that shape is made of, and a dead export is what makes review read the job as done | All seven sites now compose from `INACTIVE_STATUS_SQL` (which also closed a latent hole: they had never excluded the legacy lowercase spellings, so legacy-cased disposed rows were still being chased by the radar and the criticality engine). Containment is **CI-enforced**: a source guard fails on any `status NOT IN ('CLOSED'…)` literal in those files and asserts each references the shared constant — **comments stripped before matching**, because four earlier source guards here passed by matching the prose that described them. Mutation-verified by reverting one site |
| **BLD-010** | 08-11 15:10 | Widened the ALREADY-APPLIED migration `20260811_normalise_legacy_done_status` in place, rather than adding a new one. It had run on production 20 minutes earlier (D-22, 6 rows) | A migration file that no longer describes what the database executed. The tree would have claimed a 16-spelling repair where production ran a one-spelling repair — precisely the tree-versus-reality drift these hand-written files exist to prevent, and undetectable afterwards | Reverted the applied file untouched; the widened repair is a NEW folder (`20260811b_…`, DEF-132). **Two assertions now pin the boundary**: the applied file must not mention DEF-132 or its temp table. **Rule: an applied migration is append-only — widen by adding a file, never by editing one** |
| **BLD-007** | 08-10 | Reported wiki memory as fully indexed — *"all 12,473/12,473 pages embedded, 0 unreachable"* — measured with `count(embedding)` | The number was true and the claim was false: 63% of those vectors were `stub-768` placeholders, so retrieval was returning confident nonsense over most of Brain's memory while I reported it healthy. Cost 21 hours during which the system's own finding said otherwise every 50 minutes (DEF-118) | **A presence check is not a validity check.** Count what the column MEANS, not whether it is filled — for embeddings that is `embedding_model = <the model we search with>`. Same lesson as reporting `n_live_tup` estimates as row counts on 08-11 |

## D. Undeployed work waiting on the box

**PRUNE-002 — Self-Pruning gets its definition AND its instrument (owner ruling,
2026-09-08).** *"Self Pruning should look into aspects — 1. Database … remove all
redundant, duplications or noisy information … keep database at optimal level without loss
of any useful data. 2. Files and Codebase … review entire codebase … remove all those which
are not required any more and optimized code as per best need using current all features."*

Written into `BRAIN_VISION.md` because that is the file all three seats read, and because
the 2026-08-14 Self-Healing ruling taught what happens otherwise: a capability with a
number and no definition gets rated against the scorer's own idea of the word.

**The trap this ruling walked straight into, caught before it cost anything.** On the day
it was given, `evidence-pack.sh` carried **zero** lines about database redundancy and
**zero** about the codebase — so both aspects would have been scored `code-read` off the
new paragraph, exactly as `Learn` sat at 42 with nothing measuring it ([LEARN-002]). A
definition without an evidence line is not a capability, it is a wish, and that sentence is
now in the vision doc as a standing rule.

**Aspect 1 now measured — three faults, three tests, from the live database:**
`wiki corpus: 13,395 pages — 3,009 active, 5,643 stale, 4,743 orphan (78% of what
retrieval reads is not active)` · `duplicate person names: 415` · `contacts split across
two tables: entities 947 vs persons 974` · `heaviest dead-tuple tables: llm_spend 20,604,
criticality_fusion_cache 1,155, gate_rule_firings 969, feed_events 719`.

**415 duplicate person names against the 24 recorded on 08-31** — the junk is growing, not
holding.

**Aspect 2 now measured — from the tree, not asserted:** `live TypeScript: 659 files,
153,574 lines` · `emitted .js in dist with no matching .ts in src: 4` · `source files never
named by any import (CANDIDATES, not a verdict): 13 of 573`.

Two of those lines earn their place immediately. **The stale-artifact count caught my own
residue on its first run** — the 4 are exactly the files [PRUNE-001] deleted the day
before, proving the hazard `tsc` leaves behind is real and now visible. And the 13 orphan
candidates are named services the vision doc already lists as Class 2 — `delegationFollowUpJob`,
`delegateeFollowupWorker`, `delegateeEmailProducer` — so the heuristic finds what a human
audit found, without the audit.

**The scan is labelled a CANDIDATE list, never a verdict**, and it excludes what is never
imported BY DESIGN: `*.test.ts` (vitest discovers them), `*.d.ts` (the compiler consumes
them), and `src/scripts/` operator entry points (triaged separately by
`operatorScriptParity`). Unfiltered it reported 40 candidates whose first four were test
files — which is how a measure teaches its reader to ignore it.

**State:** `evidence-pack.sh` and `BRAIN_VISION.md` are read from source at run time, so
**the next pulse scores Self-Pruning against these numbers** with no deploy. Pack runtime
rose ~3s → ~4s. Nothing has been pruned by this entry: it makes the 36 explicable and
attackable, and the doing is the loop's work.

**OPS-002 — a pulse now builds EVERY open gap in its target capability (owner ruling,
2026-09-07: *"every build should build all open gaps"* → *"yes do it for now"*).**

**What actually limited it was not the cost cap.** `NEXEO_MAX_ITER=1` bounds *iterations*
(his 2026-08-18 ruling, after $1,144 across 162 builds). Separately, `build.md` rule 4 read
*"One change, the smallest that closes a symptom tag… not three related improvements"* —
an explicit instruction to do exactly one. Five open gaps therefore took five pulses, and
the queue grew faster than it drained.

Rule 4 now asks for every open gap **in the target capability, one commit per DEF**. The
bounds are the point, not hedges:

- **Only the target capability** — the AIM comparison and the chart row are both
  per-capability, so a turn that wanders scores MISAIMED however good the code.
- **One commit per DEF**, each naming its tag, its tests and its row. A single commit
  carrying five fixes cannot be reviewed, rolled back or attributed: Codex rules on the
  iteration, the probe rolls back the whole deploy, and the chart row asks which
  prediction came true.
- **Commit as you go, in ranking order** — the turn can end on the cap, and then everything
  already committed still ships while only the unstarted items wait. Holding finished
  fixes in the working tree is the difference between a truncated turn that delivered four
  and one that delivered none.
- **Still not a survey**, and a structural first item is finished alone rather than three
  symptoms half-patched.

**The turn cap had to move with the scope**, or the widened brief would have been a no-op
dressed as a decision: 40 turns does not fit five fixes. `NEXEO_BUILD_MAX_TURNS` now
defaults to **60** in `nightly-iteration.sh` (so the button inherits it too, per
[OPS-001]). The number is from this project's own measurement, not comfort — the worst
build ever observed ran **58 turns at $45.28** (iteration 47), so 60 is bounded by a cost
already survived once, and the mean has never been near it. The cap bounds the MAIN LOOP
only; sub-agent turns do not count against it, which is how iteration 47 logged 58 against
a limit of 40.

`review.md` is told the turn may carry several commits and to **name which commit each
finding belongs to** — a WEAK on one fix among four is far more useful than a WEAK on the
turn.

**The cost to expect, stated plainly:** a pulse that fixes four things costs more than one
that fixes one. The rail against runaway spend is still `NEXEO_MAX_ITER=1` — one pulse per
click or per night, however much it does inside that pulse.

**State:** prompts and the launcher are read from source, so this takes effect on the
**next pulse** — tonight's cron or the next click — with no deploy. Marked *"for now"* by
the owner: reverting is restoring rule 4's old paragraph and dropping the cap default.

**OPS-001 — "Heal now" runs a FULL pulse (owner decision, 2026-09-07).** He asked whether
the button runs what the nightly runs. It did not, and the difference was three phases.

`heal-now.sh` exec'd `watch-conversations.sh`, which passes `NEXEO_TARGET_SOURCE=chat`, and
inside `nexeo-loop.sh` that single flag switches off the capability **SCORE** (line ~1054),
the Codex **REVIEW** (~1934) and the **MEASURE** baseline (~824). That was correct while a
10-minute conversational cron existed and the nightly would pick those up later the same
day. **The owner disabled that cron on 2026-09-03** (`4c8ce99`) — so the button had become
the manual trigger for a schedule that no longer existed, still skipping the three phases
whose only justification was that something else would run them. **A click produced a real
build, a real deploy, and no capability card** — exactly the gap [LEARN-002] had just been
written to close.

It now execs `nightly-iteration.sh`: one implementation, so a manual run and the scheduled
run cannot behave differently. That was always this file's stated principle; it had simply
come to point at the wrong script. The click inherits the concurrency guard (one builder in
one tree, ever), the cost read back from the builder's own result event, and the rc 0/2/1
verdict line in `nightly.log`.

**The trap avoided, and it is a real one:** `NEXEO_TRIGGER` was hardcoded
`cron:nightly-iteration`, and its own comment says that stamp is where the spend deck's CRON
cut comes from. Reusing the launcher unchanged would have charged every manual click to the
cron and corrupted the one number that comment exists to protect. The trigger is now
`${NEXEO_TRIGGER:-cron:nightly-iteration}` and the button exports `manual:heal-now`.

**Consequences stated rather than discovered:** a click now takes **~30 minutes, not ~15**
(scoring and the review are the slow phases) and costs about what a night costs (~$24 mean,
$45 worst observed). The tooltip and the route docblock say so. `--build-anyway` is kept as
an accepted no-op — a full pulse always builds — and reports itself rather than being
silently ignored. The `NEXEO_WATCH_*` caps are gone with the watcher that read them; they
bounded an unattended 10-minute loop and mean nothing to a pulse a person starts.

**Verified statically, and NOT by running it** — a real run costs a night's spend: `bash -n`
clean on both scripts, the exec target present and executable, the trigger default and
override both exercised in isolation (`cron:nightly-iteration` when unset,
`manual:heal-now` when exported), no `watch-conversations.sh` reference left on the exec
path, and `tests/orchestratorPulseRoute.test.ts` **8 passed 0 failed**.

**State:** the CLIENT half (the tooltip) is live once `client/dist` is rebuilt. The scripts
take effect on the **next click** — they are read from source, not from `dist`, so they need
no deploy. The route docblock ships with the next nightly.

**LEARN-001 — the approval gate is narrowed, not removed (owner decision, 2026-09-07).**
Owner: *"then make it human intervention less."*

**The gate was unconditional, and its own docblock said otherwise.** `proposeMemory`
computed `requiresApproval = sensitive || critical || createdByBrain`, and
`createdByBrain` **defaults to `true`** — so every memory required a click and the
"user-explicit low/normal memories can land active" path the comment promised could never
fire for anything. Two governed memories sat in `pending_approval` from **2026-07-14** and
**2026-08-07**, `Active Memories (0)`, and `Learn` scored **42** as the only capability
with zero movement all campaign. **The score was measuring two unclicked buttons.**

**What self-activates now:** `sourceType = 'chat_standing_preference'` **and** confidence
**>= 0.9** **and** sensitivity `low`/`normal`. That source earns it because
`standingPreferenceCapture` already spends three filters first — a durability-marker
pre-filter (always / never / from now on / hamesha…), a deliberately conservative LLM
distill told to reject one-off requests, questions and single-value corrections, and a
0.6 floor below which the candidate is dropped. What survives is a rule the owner said
out loud. The bar for activating is **higher (0.9) than the bar for proposing (0.6)**,
because activating without a human is the stronger act.

**What still needs his click, and this is the half that must not drift:** anything
`sensitive` or `critical`, at any confidence, forever. Anything below 0.9. **Any source
without those filters in front of it — the correction distiller included.** DEF-149 is
why that is a whitelist and not a boolean: *"chase her again"* was once filed as a
preference when it was an imperative to act, and that message carries no durability
marker, so it exits the pre-filter for free and never reaches this decision.

**Reversibility is what makes it acceptable.** A self-activated row is distinguishable in
the database — `approved_at` set with `approved_by` **NULL**: active, and no human put it
there. It shows in Active Memories and the audit feed, and one click archives it.

**Evidence:** `tests/standingPreferenceAutoActivation.test.ts`, **9 passed 0 failed**, and
**5 mutants applied, all 5 die** — dropping the sensitive/critical guard kills 2, dropping
the confidence bar kills 1, allowing any source kills 3, dropping the `approvedAt` stamp
kills 1, raising the bar above 1.0 kills 3.

**LEARN-002 — the sweep, the phase, and the evidence (same day, owner: *"since learning
and improvement is already part of brain's capability that's why i am expecting to make it
part of brain's capability (nightly cron) and also update score of brain's capability"*).**

LEARN-001 only helped memories created AFTER it existed, so anything captured earlier
stayed pending forever and still needed a hand — the original complaint, one layer down.
Three pieces close it:

1. **`sweepQualifyingMemories()`** (`learningService.ts`) — activates every pending row
   that clears the bar, reading the SAME two exported constants as the creation-time rule,
   because a sweep with its own copy of the rule is how a gate ends up wider in the
   background than in the foreground. Idempotent: it reads only `pending_approval`, so a
   second run the same night finds nothing.
2. **A LEARN phase in the nightly** (`nexeo-loop.sh`), running `nexeoLearnSweep.ts`
   **before** the scorer — the card is written Pre-Build, so a sweep running afterwards
   would not appear in tonight's numbers, and the point is that the score moves in the
   same pulse that does the work. A failure there never aborts the pulse.
3. **Learn evidence in `evidence-pack.sh`, which had NONE.** Its only memory line queried
   `brain_memory`, a table that does not exist — the scorer's own notes read *"pack:
   brain_memory absent"* — so Learn was scored `code-read` every time, on the shape of the
   code rather than on whether anything was ever learned. **It was not being measured.**
   The pack now reports the active/pending split, the self-activated count, and the age of
   the oldest row still waiting on a human.

**RESULT, measured not claimed.** The sweep ran: `scanned 2 pending · activated 2 · held
0`. The pack now reads **"2 ACTIVE (injected into every prompt), 0 pending a human"** and
**"self-activated by Brain: 2 of 2 active"**, where before today it read 0 active and
`oldest pending 2026-07-14`.

**This half is LIVE with no deploy.** `renderGovernedMemoriesBlock` reads the table at
prompt time and the running server already contains it, so *"always send text instead of
voice notes"* and *"use asad.ahmed@tmcltd.com as the email address"* are steering replies
now. Either is one click from archived in the Learning tab.

**Evidence:** `tests/standingPreferenceAutoActivation.test.ts` **18 passed 0 failed**;
**11 mutants applied, all 11 die** (5 on the creation gate, 6 on the sweep — including
attributing a swept row to a human, and reading a status other than pending).

**State:** the CODE is committed and **NOT deployed** — the gate, the sweep, the LEARN
phase and the pack evidence ship with the next nightly. The DATA change above is already
live.

**PRUNE-001 — the Self-Improvement pipeline is retired (owner decision, 2026-09-07).**
Owner: *"we agreed to retire or remove it."* Removed: `gapDetectionService.ts`,
`productProposalService.ts`, `developmentRequestService.ts`, `jobs/gapDetectionJob.ts`,
`BrainImprovementTab.jsx`, its 24h sweep in `server.ts`, and 123 lines of admin routes
(the `/gaps`, `/proposals` and `/development-requests` families) from `learningRoutes.ts`.

**Why it went, on the evidence:** it was the second live owner of one concern — detect a
gap, decide a fix, request the work. In 79 days it produced **1 gap** (2026-06-20, still
`new`), **0 product proposals** and **0 development requests**, and nothing downstream ever
read its output: `productProposalService` and `developmentRequestService` were imported by
the admin routes and by nothing else, and the night cron aims from `chat-root-causes.md`
and the capability card, never from `brain_development_requests`. The other owner has
shipped ~56 defects from his actual chats. `BRAIN_VISION.md`'s rule for that shape is
collapse, not keep.

**What was deliberately NOT removed.** (i) The **Learning** tab and the `/memories`,
`/audit` and `/interactions` routes — that is the separate **Learn** capability, its read
path is live (`renderGovernedMemoriesBlock` is injected into every prompt by
`brainVoiceContext` and `brainComposer`), and two governed memories are pending approval
with that panel as their only surface. (ii) The tables `brain_detected_gaps`,
`brain_product_proposals`, `brain_development_requests` and their Prisma models — dropping
them is irreversible, they cost nothing, and the single row in them is the only record the
pipeline ever ran.

**State:** the CLIENT half is LIVE (`client/dist` rebuilt 2026-09-07, apache serves it, the
tab is gone). The SERVER half — the killed sweep and the unmounted routes — is committed and
**NOT deployed**; it ships with the next nightly deploy. Nothing calls those routes now that
the tab is gone, so the order is safe. **One thing to check at that deploy:** `tsc` does not
delete output for a deleted input, so `server/dist` will keep stale `gapDetectionService.js`,
`productProposalService.js`, `developmentRequestService.js` and `gapDetectionJob.js` unless
the deploy clears them — nothing imports them, but they will sit in the manifest.

All of `DEF-018`, `DEF-023`, `DEF-024`, `DEF-025` were **deployed 2026-08-05 11:40** at HEAD
`7ace0f5`. Nothing is waiting on the box.

Awaiting live exercise: does "send" dispatch (DEF-024), do previews name items (DEF-018),
do plan steps update (DEF-023), does native typing appear (DEF-025). `DEF-024` includes a migration
(`20260804_pending_unique_any_name`), so the deploy needs `prisma migrate deploy`.
